- [ ] **Step 2: Implement in `cmd-init.ts`**

- Export:

  ```ts
  /**
   * The mode for an install without state.json: an upgrade (a previous
   * install, or data from one) stays on HTTP until the operator enables
   * HTTPS — unless that data was already served over HTTPS (the marker the
   * server writes), so a lost TLS folder never silently falls back to HTTP.
   */
  export function decideInitialMode(input: {
    previousInstall: boolean;
    dbExists: boolean;
    markerExists: boolean;
  }): StateMode {
    return (input.previousInstall || input.dbExists) && !input.markerExists
      ? 'legacy-http'
      : 'https';
  }
  ```

- Add options `mode: { type: 'string' }`, `'first-install': { type: 'boolean' }`, `quiet: { type: 'boolean' }`. Only `auto` is a valid `--mode` (a `UsageError` otherwise). `--quiet` skips `printCa` (the sidecar prints it itself).
- Rename the current body to `async function material(ctx, values): Promise<number>`, and in `init`:

  ```ts
  if (values.mode === undefined) return material(ctx, values);
  if (readState(ctx.layout) !== undefined) return material(ctx, values); // mode already chosen: fill in only
  const db = ctx.env['PIPULSE_DB_PATH']?.trim() || '/var/lib/pipulse/pipulse.sqlite';
  const exists = (path: string, whenUnsure: boolean) => {
    try {
      statSync(path);
      return true;
    } catch (error) {
      return (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT' ? false : whenUnsure;
    }
  };
  // Unsure (unreadable) means: data present, never served over HTTPS — the choice that keeps HTTP.
  const markerExists = exists(join(dirname(db), 'tls-installed'), false);
  const mode = decideInitialMode({
    previousInstall: !values['first-install'],
    dbExists: exists(db, true),
    markerExists
  });
  const hadCa = checkCa(paths(ctx.layout).caDir).kind === 'ok';
  let code: number;
  try {
    code = await material(ctx, values);
  } catch (error) {
    // e.g. openssl missing: an upgrade must still land on legacy-http.
    ctx.err(`pipulse tls: ${(error as Error).message}`);
    code = 1;
  }
  if (mode === 'https') {
    if (code !== 0) return code; // no state.json: the server refuses rather than serve something half-made
    if (markerExists && !hadCa)
      ctx.err(
        'warning: this data was served over HTTPS before but its TLS folder is gone: a new CA was made, so every device must trust the new CA (sudo pipulse tls export-ca)'
      );
    writeState(ctx.layout, 'https', ctx.hook);
    return 0;
  }
  if (code !== 0)
    ctx.err(
      'warning: HTTPS is not prepared (see above); PiPulse stays on plain HTTP. Try again later with: sudo pipulse tls init'
    );
  writeState(ctx.layout, 'legacy-http', ctx.hook);
  return 0;
  ```

- [ ] **Step 3: `lib.sh` and `setup.sh`**

`packaging/lib.sh`, append:

```sh
# pipulse_health — the installed version's shared health check (HTTP, or verified HTTPS)
# with only the settings it reads, each through env_value (pipulse.env is data). Sets HEALTH_MSG.
HEALTH_MSG=
pipulse_health() {
  HEALTH_MSG=
  i=0
  while [ "$i" -lt "${PIPULSE_HEALTH_WAIT:-30}" ]; do
    rc=0
    HEALTH_MSG=$(env -i PATH="$PATH" \
      PIPULSE_HOST="$(env_value PIPULSE_HOST)" PIPULSE_PORT="$(env_value PIPULSE_PORT)" \
      PIPULSE_TLS="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS)" PIPULSE_TLS_DIR="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS_DIR)" \
      PIPULSE_TLS_CERT="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS_CERT)" PIPULSE_TLS_KEY="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS_KEY)" \
      PIPULSE_TLS_CA="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS_CA)" PIPULSE_TLS_NAMES="$(env_value PIPULSE_TLS_NAMES)" \
      /opt/pipulse/node/bin/node /opt/pipulse/app/packages/tls/dist/health-check.js 2>&1 >/dev/null) || rc=$?
    HEALTH_MSG=${HEALTH_MSG#'[pipulse] health check: '}
    [ "$rc" -eq 0 ] && return 0
    [ "$rc" -eq 2 ] && return 2
    i=$((i + 1)); sleep 1
  done
  return 1
}
```

`packaging/setup.sh`, a new step between 3 (settings) and 4 (service):

```sh
# 3b. HTTPS material and mode: made and checked before the service (re)starts;
#     state.json is written last, and an upgrade never switches the transport.
# install.sh leaves this marker on a fresh install until setup succeeds, so a
# retry after any failure is still a first install, never an HTTP "upgrade".
pending=/etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending
[ -e "$pending" ] && first=yes
tls_ready=no
tls_cli() {
  n=/opt/pipulse/node/bin/node
  [ -x "$n" ] || n=$(command -v node)
  "$n" --disable-warning=ExperimentalWarning "$here/../packages/tls/dist/cli.js" "$@"
}
# --yes: consent to a subnet came from the installer's prompt or PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS.
set -- init --mode auto --yes
[ "$first" = yes ] && set -- "$@" --first-install
if [ "$first" = yes ] && [ -n "${PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET:-}" ]; then
  set -- "$@" --subnet "$PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET"
fi
if tls_cli "$@"; then
  tls_ready=yes
else
  if [ "$first" = yes ] && [ -n "${PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET:-}" ] && tls_cli init --mode auto --yes --first-install; then
    tls_ready=yes
    warn "$PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET was not accepted; the CA covers names only (to add a subnet later: sudo pipulse tls new-ca --subnet <cidr>)"
  elif [ "$first" = yes ]; then
    die 'HTTPS could not be set up (see above). Fix it and run setup again: sudo sh /opt/pipulse/app/packaging/setup.sh --first-install — or serve plain HTTP: sudo pipulse tls disable --allow-insecure'
  fi
fi
```

and at the end of step 4, for a first install where the service was started:

```sh
  if [ "$first" = yes ] && [ "$(systemctl is-active pipulse 2>/dev/null || true)" = active ]; then
    if pipulse_health; then
      # The marker (Task 21) is part of success: without it, losing the TLS folder
      # later would make this HTTPS install look like an HTTP one.
      db=$(env_value PIPULSE_DB_PATH)
      marker="$(dirname "${db:-/var/lib/pipulse/pipulse.sqlite}")/tls-installed"
      i=0
      while [ ! -e "$marker" ] && [ "$i" -lt 5 ]; do i=$((i + 1)); sleep 1; done
      if grep -q '"https"' /etc/pipulse/tls/state.json 2>/dev/null && [ ! -e "$marker" ]; then
        printf '[pipulse] error: the server serves HTTPS but could not write %s (see journalctl -u pipulse); fix the data folder, then: sudo sh /opt/pipulse/app/packaging/setup.sh --first-install\n' "$marker" >&2
        exit 1
      fi
      rm -f "$pending"
      log "PiPulse is running: https://$(hostname):${port:-8889} (trust its CA first: sudo pipulse tls export-ca)"
    else
      printf '[pipulse] error: PiPulse did not come up healthy%s\n' "${HEALTH_MSG:+ ($HEALTH_MSG)}" >&2
      printf '  see: journalctl -u pipulse   and   sudo pipulse tls status\n' >&2
      printf '  retry: sudo sh /opt/pipulse/app/packaging/setup.sh --first-install\n  or serve plain HTTP: sudo pipulse tls disable --allow-insecure\n' >&2
      exit 1
    fi
  fi
```

(`port` is computed in step 5 today. Move its two lines up to the start of step 4.)

When the service isn't started here (`--no-start`, or no systemd), made material and a written `state.json` are the success gate. After step 4 add:

```sh
if [ "$first" = yes ] && [ "$tls_ready" = yes ] && { [ "$start" = no ] || [ ! -d /run/systemd/system ]; }; then
  rm -f "$pending"
fi
```

**The pending marker in `packaging/install.sh`.** `install_tarball` used to decide "first install" from the app folder alone. So a first run that deployed the app and then failed (in setup, or before setup ran) was retried as an _upgrade_, and landed on `legacy-http`. Now, before the new app is moved into place:

```sh
  # A fresh install stays fresh until setup has made HTTPS work (setup removes this).
  pending=/etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending
  if [ ! -d /opt/pipulse/app ] && [ ! -e "$(db_path)" ] && [ ! -e /etc/pipulse/tls ]; then
    install -d -m 750 /etc/pipulse
    (umask 077 && : > "$pending")
  fi
```

Where `first` is computed:

```sh
  first=yes
  [ "$had_previous" = no ] || first=no
  [ -e /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending ] && first=yes
```

Pass it on every path to `setup.sh`: the `--no-start` path (`if [ "$first" = yes ]; then sh …/setup.sh --no-start --first-install; else sh …/setup.sh --no-start; fi`) and `start_service`'s `PIPULSE_FORCE_RESTART` branch, which had the same gap (`start_service` already gets `$first` as `$1`). A health failure on a pending fresh install never "rolls back" to the half-made previous run: the rollback condition becomes `[ "$had_previous" = yes ] && [ ! -e /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending ] || die "PiPulse did not become healthy…"`. `--purge` removes the marker with `/etc/pipulse`. The `.deb` needs no marker: `postinst configure` gets an empty previous version until a configure succeeds, so `apt-get -f install` retries as a first install already.

H4 (the installer's post-install probe after a fresh HTTPS install), traced: `health_ok` runs the installed version's shared check under `env -i` with `env_value` settings. The check reads `state.json` and verifies against `ca.crt`, so no new code is needed. The one gap is that the `PIPULSE_FORCE_RESTART` (test) path didn't pass `--first-install`, which is fixed above. Section 9 of the test below proves both transports.

- [ ] **Step 4: Packaging tests**

`packaging/test/migrate-tls.test.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# Mode selection on install and upgrade (spec "Setup and migration"): fresh → https,
# upgrade → legacy-http with HTTPS prepared, state.json never touched, lost folder + marker → https.
set -eu
repo=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/../.." && pwd)
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
out=$(mktemp -d)
"$repo/packaging/build-tarball.sh" 0.0.1 "$out" >/dev/null
"$repo/packaging/build-tarball.sh" 0.0.2 "$out" >/dev/null
fail=0
ok() { echo "ok - $1"; }
bad() { echo "not ok - $1"; fail=1; }
state() { sed -n 's/.*"mode":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' /etc/pipulse/tls/state.json 2>/dev/null; }
fp() { openssl x509 -in /etc/pipulse/tls/ca.crt -noout -fingerprint -sha256 2>/dev/null; }
# No timesyncd here: an unknown clock is accepted for making a CA (with a warning).

# 1. Fresh install.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/i.log 2>&1 || { cat /tmp/i.log; bad 'fresh install ran'; }
[ "$(state)" = https ] && ok 'fresh install selects https' || bad "fresh install selects https (got $(state))"
grep -q 'CA SHA-256 fingerprint:' /tmp/i.log && ok 'fresh install prints the CA fingerprint' || bad 'fresh install prints the CA fingerprint'
grep -q 'IP access .* is not covered' /tmp/i.log && ok 'and says IP access is not covered' || bad 'and says IP access is not covered'
first=$(fp)

# 2. Upgrade with state.json: untouched, same CA.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.2.tar.gz" --no-start >/dev/null 2>&1
[ "$(state)" = https ] && [ "$(fp)" = "$first" ] && ok 'upgrade keeps state.json and the CA' || bad 'upgrade keeps state.json and the CA'

# 3. Upgrade from a pre-6b install (no /etc/pipulse/tls, data present).
rm -rf /etc/pipulse/tls /var/lib/pipulse/tls-installed
echo data > /var/lib/pipulse/pipulse.sqlite
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/u.log 2>&1
[ "$(state)" = legacy-http ] && ok 'pre-6b upgrade stays on HTTP' || bad "pre-6b upgrade stays on HTTP (got $(state))"
[ -e /etc/pipulse/tls/leaf.pem ] && ok 'and HTTPS is prepared' || bad 'and HTTPS is prepared'
grep -q 'sudo pipulse tls enable' /tmp/u.log && ok 'and says how to enable it' || bad 'and says how to enable it'

# 4. Reinstall over kept data after --uninstall of a pre-6b version: still HTTP.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --uninstall >/dev/null
rm -rf /etc/pipulse/tls
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start >/dev/null 2>&1
[ "$(state)" = legacy-http ] && ok 'a reinstall over old data stays on HTTP' || bad "a reinstall over old data stays on HTTP (got $(state))"

# 5. The TLS folder is lost on data that was served over HTTPS: back to https, new CA, warned.
rm -rf /etc/pipulse/tls
touch /var/lib/pipulse/tls-installed
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.2.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/l.log 2>&1
[ "$(state)" = https ] && grep -q 'every device must trust the new CA' /tmp/l.log && ok 'a lost TLS folder on HTTPS data goes back to https, with a warning' || bad 'lost TLS folder'

# 6. The installer's subnet answer reaches init on a fresh install.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null
PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET=192.168.77.0/24 sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start >/dev/null 2>&1
grep -q '192.168.77.0/24' /etc/pipulse/tls/ca-meta.json && ok 'an accepted subnet is in the CA' || bad 'an accepted subnet is in the CA'
[ ! -e /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending ] && ok 'a successful fresh install leaves no pending marker' || bad 'a successful fresh install leaves no pending marker'

# 7. A fresh install whose HTTPS setup fails is retried as a fresh install, and settings
#    that exist only in pipulse.env (not the environment) reach setup's init.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null
install -d -m 750 /etc/pipulse
printf 'PIPULSE_TLS_DIR=/srv/pipulse-tls\nPIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS=192.168.77.0/24\n' > /etc/pipulse/pipulse.env
if sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/f.log 2>&1; then bad 'a refused TLS folder fails the fresh install'; else ok 'a refused TLS folder fails the fresh install'; fi
grep -q 'PIPULSE_TLS_DIR=/srv/pipulse-tls' /tmp/f.log && [ ! -e /srv/pipulse-tls ] && ok 'setup refuses a PIPULSE_TLS_DIR set only in pipulse.env' || bad 'setup refuses a PIPULSE_TLS_DIR set only in pipulse.env'
[ -e /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending ] && ok 'the failed fresh install stays pending' || bad 'the failed fresh install stays pending'
sed -i '/PIPULSE_TLS_DIR/d' /etc/pipulse/pipulse.env
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/r.log 2>&1 || { cat /tmp/r.log; bad 'the retry ran'; }
[ "$(state)" = https ] && ok 'the retry is still a fresh install (https)' || bad "the retry is still a fresh install (got $(state))"
grep -q '192.168.77.0/24' /etc/pipulse/tls/ca-meta.json && ok 'PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS from pipulse.env alone is honoured' || bad 'PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS from pipulse.env alone is honoured'
[ ! -e /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending ] && ok 'the pending marker goes once it worked' || bad 'the pending marker goes once it worked'

# 8. A first run that died after deploying the app, before setup ran: the retry is fresh too.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null
mkdir -p /opt/pipulse/app /opt/pipulse/node && echo '{"version":"0.0.0"}' > /opt/pipulse/app/version.json
install -d -m 750 /etc/pipulse && : > /etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" --no-start > /tmp/d.log 2>&1 || { cat /tmp/d.log; bad 'the retry after an early death ran'; }
[ "$(state)" = https ] && ok 'a retry after dying before setup is a fresh install' || bad "a retry after dying before setup is a fresh install (got $(state))"

# 9. The installer's own post-install probe (the shared check under env -i): over verified TLS
#    after a fresh HTTPS install, over HTTP with PIPULSE_TLS=off. The stand-in restart starts
#    the real server the way the unit would.
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null
cat > /usr/local/bin/pipulse-test-restart <<'SH'
#!/bin/sh
pkill -f /opt/pipulse/app/packages/api/dist/server.js || true
sleep 1
setpriv --reuid=pipulse --regid=pipulse --init-groups sh -c 'set -a; . /etc/pipulse/pipulse.env; set +a; cd /var/lib/pipulse; PIPULSE_DB_PATH=/var/lib/pipulse/pipulse.sqlite exec /opt/pipulse/node/bin/node --disable-warning=ExperimentalWarning /opt/pipulse/app/packages/api/dist/server.js' >> /tmp/probe-server.log 2>&1 &
SH
chmod +x /usr/local/bin/pipulse-test-restart
: > /tmp/probe-server.log
probe() { PIPULSE_FORCE_RESTART=1 PIPULSE_RESTART_CMD=/usr/local/bin/pipulse-test-restart PIPULSE_STOP_CMD='pkill -f /opt/pipulse/app/packages/api/dist/server.js' PIPULSE_HEALTH_WAIT=40 sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --from "$1" > /tmp/probe.log 2>&1; }
probe "$out/pipulse-0.0.1.tar.gz" || cat /tmp/probe.log
grep -q 'running 0.0.1' /tmp/probe.log && grep -q 'listening on https://' /tmp/probe-server.log && ok 'the post-install probe passes over verified TLS' || bad 'the post-install probe passes over verified TLS'
[ -e /var/lib/pipulse/tls-installed ] && ok 'the server marked its data as served over HTTPS' || bad 'the server marked its data as served over HTTPS'
echo 'PIPULSE_TLS=off' >> /etc/pipulse/pipulse.env
probe "$out/pipulse-0.0.2.tar.gz" || cat /tmp/probe.log
grep -q 'running 0.0.2' /tmp/probe.log && grep -q 'listening on http://' /tmp/probe-server.log && ok 'with PIPULSE_TLS=off the probe goes over HTTP' || bad 'with PIPULSE_TLS=off the probe goes over HTTP'
pkill -f /opt/pipulse/app/packages/api/dist/server.js || true
rm -f /usr/local/bin/pipulse-test-restart
sh "$repo/packaging/install.sh" --purge >/dev/null
exit $fail
```

### Task 23: The installer asks about IP access (interactive only)

**Files:**

- Modify: `packaging/install.sh`
- Test: `packaging/test/migrate-tls.test.sh` (the helper functions)

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: `network_of CIDR`, `candidate_subnet`, `ask_subnet` in `install.sh`. `ask_subnet` exports `PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET` when the operator says yes. apt passes the environment through to `postinst` → `setup.sh`, and the tarball path runs `setup.sh` as a child.

Rules (spec "Installer modes"): interactive only on a **fresh** install (no `/opt/pipulse/app/version.json`, no `/etc/pipulse/tls/state.json`), when `/dev/tty` can be opened and the script isn't running under apt. It detects one candidate: the default-route interface's IPv4 network, skipping bridges and VPNs. It shows the consequence text and asks with default **No**. No terminal, or an upgrade: never waits, and the output from `init` already carries the follow-up `new-ca --subnet` command.

- [ ] **Step 1: Implement**

In `packaging/install.sh`, before `main()`:

```sh
# network_of 192.168.1.35/24 → 192.168.1.0/24
network_of() {
  echo "$1" | awk -F'[./]' '{ p = $5; ip = (($1 * 256 + $2) * 256 + $3) * 256 + $4; b = 2 ^ (32 - p); n = ip - (ip % b);
    printf "%d.%d.%d.%d/%d\n", int(n / 16777216) % 256, int(n / 65536) % 256, int(n / 256) % 256, n % 256, p }'
}

# The default-route interface's IPv4 network, unless it is a bridge or VPN.
candidate_subnet() {
  dev=$(ip -4 route show default 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/.* dev \([^ ]*\).*/\1/p' | head -n 1)
  [ -n "$dev" ] || return 1
  case $dev in docker* | br-* | veth* | tun* | wg*) return 1 ;; esac
  cidr=$(ip -4 -o addr show dev "$dev" scope global 2>/dev/null | awk '{ print $4 }' | head -n 1)
  [ -n "$cidr" ] || return 1
  network_of "$cidr"
}

# Fresh install with a terminal: offer IP access for the LAN (default No).
ask_subnet() {
  [ -e /opt/pipulse/app/version.json ] && return 0
  [ -e /etc/pipulse/tls/state.json ] && return 0
  (: </dev/tty) 2>/dev/null || return 0
  cidr=$(candidate_subnet) || return 0
  cat >/dev/tty <<EOF

[pipulse] HTTPS: PiPulse makes its own certificate authority (CA) for this Pi.
By default it covers the Pi's names ($(hostname), $(hostname).local, localhost), not IP addresses.

This CA will be trusted for the following DNS names and IP ranges. Anyone holding its private key can impersonate hosts within those ranges.

Accepting $cidr allows this CA to issue certificates for any IP in that subnet. A stolen CA key could impersonate other devices there.

EOF
  printf 'Also cover https://<IP address> in %s? [y/N] ' "$cidr" >/dev/tty
  answer=
  read -r answer </dev/tty || answer=
  case $answer in
    y | Y | yes | YES) PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET=$cidr; export PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET ;;
    *) printf '[pipulse] names only; to add it later: sudo pipulse tls new-ca --subnet %s\n' "$cidr" >/dev/tty ;;
  esac
}
```

In `main()`, call `ask_subnet` for the `apt` and `tarball` modes, before installing (after the root check). Don't call it for `--no-start` (unattended by definition) or for uninstall and purge.

In `install_apt`, `apt-get install … pipulse` inherits the exported variable, and `postinst` passes it to `setup.sh`. Nothing else to change. For clarity, add a comment next to the install line: `# PIPULSE_TLS_INIT_SUBNET (from ask_subnet) reaches setup.sh through dpkg's environment`.

```sh
# shellcheck source=/dev/null

[ "$(network_of 192.168.1.35/24)" = 192.168.1.0/24 ] && [ "$(network_of 10.20.30.40/16)" = 10.20.0.0/16 ] && ok 'network_of' || bad 'network_of'
```

Then purge and run `sh packaging/install.sh --from <tarball>` with `PIPULSE_FORCE_RESTART=1 PIPULSE_RESTART_CMD=true`: the prompt appears, `y` puts the subnet in `ca-meta.json`, and Enter keeps names only.

### Task 24: The Docker sidecar commands

**Files:**

- Create: `packages/tls/src/sidecar.ts`, `packages/tls/test/sidecar.test.ts`, `packages/tls/test/module-graph.test.ts`
- Modify: `packages/tls/src/cli.ts` (three `case`s)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `init` (`--yes --quiet`), `renew`, `decideInitialMode`, `printCa`, `checkCa`, `checkLeaf`, `readRenewStatus`, `ensureDir`, `MODES`.
- Produces:
  - `READY_FILE = '/tmp/pipulse-sidecar-ready'` (the sidecar's tmpfs, so a restart starts unready), `SIDECAR_INTERVAL_MS = 6 h`
  - `sidecarInit(ctx): number`: `/tls` `root:pipulse 2750`, `/tls-ca` `root 0700` (needs `CHOWN`, nothing else)
  - `sidecarStart(ctx, readyFile?): Promise<number>`: recover, stage, validate, activate (through `init`), write `state.json` if absent (the legacy marker rule), print the CA facts, mark ready. It fails before readiness when something is wrong.
  - `sidecar(ctx, args, options?: { readyFile?: string; intervalMs?: number; stop?: AbortSignal }): Promise<number>`: `sidecarStart`, then `renew` now and every 6 h
  - `sidecarHealth(ctx, readyFile?): number`: 0 when ready, the leaf verifies and hasn't expired, and the last renewal isn't `failed` (`waiting-clock` is healthy); else 1

- [ ] **Step 1: Failing tests**

`packages/tls/test/sidecar.test.ts`:

```ts
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { main } from '../src/cli.js';
import { paths, readMeta, readState, writeRenewStatus } from '../src/layout.js';
import { sidecar, sidecarHealth, sidecarStart } from '../src/sidecar.js';
import { testContext, type TestContext } from './cli-context.js';
import { tempDir } from './helpers.js';

let dir: string;
let ready: string;
const docker = (overrides = {}): TestContext => {
  const ctx = testContext(join(dir, 'tls'), { inContainer: true, ...overrides });
  ctx.layout = { ...ctx.layout, caRoot: join(dir, 'tls-ca') };
  ctx.env['PIPULSE_DB_PATH'] = join(dir, 'data', 'pipulse.sqlite');
  return ctx;
};
beforeEach(() => {
  dir = tempDir();
  for (const d of ['tls', 'tls-ca', 'data']) mkdirSync(join(dir, d));
  ready = join(dir, 'ready');
});
afterEach(() => rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }));

describe('sidecarStart', () => {
  it('first start: CA in the CA volume only, leaf and https state in /tls, then ready', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    expect(await sidecarStart(ctx, ready)).toBe(0);
    expect(existsSync(join(dir, 'tls-ca', 'ca', 'ca.key'))).toBe(true);
    expect(readdirSync(join(dir, 'tls'))).not.toContain('ca');
    expect(readState(ctx.layout)).toBe('https');
    expect(existsSync(ready)).toBe(true);
    expect(ctx.lines.filter((l) => l.startsWith('CA SHA-256 fingerprint:'))).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it('prints the same CA facts on every start', async () => {
    const first = docker();
    await sidecarStart(first, ready);
    const second = docker();
    await sidecarStart(second, ready);
    const facts = (c: TestContext) =>
      c.lines.filter((l) => /^CA SHA-256|^ {2}PiPulse CA|^ {2}(DNS|IP):/.test(l));
    expect(facts(second)).toEqual(facts(first));
  });

  it('existing data without the marker is an upgrade (legacy-http); with it, https', async () => {
    writeFileSync(join(dir, 'data', 'pipulse.sqlite'), '');
    const ctx = docker();
    await sidecarStart(ctx, ready);
    expect(readState(ctx.layout)).toBe('legacy-http');
    expect(ctx.lines.join('\n')).toContain(
      'docker compose run --rm pipulse-tls pipulse tls enable --yes'
    );
    rmSync(join(dir, 'tls', 'state.json'));
    writeFileSync(join(dir, 'data', 'tls-installed'), '');
    await sidecarStart(docker(), ready);
    expect(readState(ctx.layout)).toBe('https');
  });

  it('takes subnets from PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS only (never inferred)', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    ctx.env['PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS'] = '192.168.1.0/24';
    await sidecarStart(ctx, ready);
    expect(readMeta(ctx.layout)!.constraints.subnets).toEqual(['192.168.1.0/24']);
  });

  it('is not ready when it can’t issue (clock unsynced)', async () => {
    const ctx = docker({ clock: () => ({ state: 'unsynced', synced: false }) });
    expect(await sidecarStart(ctx, ready)).toBe(1);
    expect(existsSync(ready)).toBe(false);
  });

  it('new-ca works across the two volumes', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    await sidecarStart(ctx, ready);
    const before = readMeta(ctx.layout)!.fingerprint;
    const run = docker();
    run.setNow(run.now() + 5000);
    expect(await main(['new-ca', '--yes'], run)).toBe(0);
    expect(readMeta(ctx.layout)!.fingerprint).not.toBe(before);
    expect(readdirSync(join(dir, 'tls-ca')).some((n) => n.startsWith('ca.old-'))).toBe(true);
  });
});

describe('sidecarHealth', () => {
  it('is unhealthy before ready, healthy after, unhealthy when renewal failed', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    expect(sidecarHealth(ctx, ready)).toBe(1);
    await sidecarStart(ctx, ready);
    expect(sidecarHealth(ctx, ready)).toBe(0);
    writeRenewStatus(ctx.layout, {
      version: 1,
      lastAttempt: ctx.now(),
      result: 'waiting-clock',
      reason: 'clock unknown'
    });
    expect(sidecarHealth(ctx, ready)).toBe(0);
    writeRenewStatus(ctx.layout, {
      version: 1,
      lastAttempt: ctx.now(),
      result: 'failed',
      reason: 'x'
    });
    expect(sidecarHealth(ctx, ready)).toBe(1);
  });

  it('is unhealthy once the leaf has expired', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    await sidecarStart(ctx, ready);
    ctx.setNow(ctx.now() + 100 * 86_400_000);
    expect(sidecarHealth(ctx, ready)).toBe(1);
  });
});

describe('sidecar loop', () => {
  it('starts, renews once, and stops when told to', async () => {
    const ctx = docker();
    const stop = new AbortController();
    const running = sidecar(ctx, [], { readyFile: ready, intervalMs: 50, stop: stop.signal });
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 200));
    stop.abort();
    expect(await running).toBe(0);
    expect(existsSync(paths(ctx.layout).renewStatus)).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

`packages/tls/test/module-graph.test.ts`:

```ts
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

const SRC = fileURLToPath(new URL('../src/', import.meta.url));

/** The static (non-type) imports reachable from `entry`. Dynamic import() is left out on purpose. */
function graph(entry: string) {
  const seen = new Set<string>();
  const builtins = new Map<string, string[]>();
  const queue = [join(SRC, entry)];
  while (queue.length > 0) {
    const file = queue.pop()!;
    if (seen.has(file)) continue;
    seen.add(file);
    const text = readFileSync(file, 'utf8');
    for (const match of text.matchAll(/^(import|export)\s(?!type\s)[^;]*?from\s+'([^']+)'/gms)) {
      const spec = match[2]!;
      if (spec.startsWith('node:')) builtins.set(spec, [...(builtins.get(spec) ?? []), match[0]]);
      else if (spec.startsWith('.'))
        queue.push(resolve(dirname(file), spec.replace(/\.js$/, '.ts')));
    }
  }
  return { files: [...seen], builtins };
}

describe('the sidecar opens no sockets', () => {
  it.each(['sidecar.ts', 'cli.ts'])(
    '%s has no network client in its static module graph',
    (entry) => {
      const { files, builtins } = graph(entry);
      for (const forbidden of ['node:http', 'node:https', 'node:http2', 'node:dgram']) {
        expect(builtins.has(forbidden), `${entry} reaches ${forbidden}`).toBe(false);
      }
      for (const statement of builtins.get('node:net') ?? [])
        expect(statement).toMatch(/^import \{ isIP \} from 'node:net'/);
      for (const file of files)
        expect(readFileSync(file, 'utf8')).not.toMatch(/\bfetch\(|new WebSocket\b/);
    }
  );
});
```

(The `strace` half of this guarantee is in `tls.test.sh`, Task 12.)

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `sidecar.ts`**

```ts
import { existsSync, rmSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { parseArgs } from 'node:util';
import { printCa, usage, type Context } from './cli-common.js';
import { decideInitialMode, init } from './cmd-init.js';
import { renew } from './cmd-renew.js';
import { ensureDir } from './files.js';
import { withLock } from './lock.js';
import { validityOf } from './inspect.js';
import { MODES, paths, priv, pub, readRenewStatus, readState, writeState } from './layout.js';
import { checkCa, checkLeaf } from './material.js';

export const READY_FILE = '/tmp/pipulse-sidecar-ready';
const lockOptions = (ctx: Context, create: boolean) => ({
  create,
  waitMs: ctx.lockWaitMs,
  now: ctx.now,
  sleep: ctx.sleep
});
export const SIDECAR_INTERVAL_MS = 6 * 3_600_000;

/** The one-shot init service (CAP_CHOWN only): the two volumes' owners and modes. */
export function sidecarInit(ctx: Context): number {
  ensureDir(ctx.layout.tlsDir, { mode: MODES.tlsDir, owner: pub(ctx.layout) });
  ensureDir(ctx.layout.caRoot, { mode: MODES.caRoot, owner: priv(ctx.layout) });
  ctx.out(`${ctx.layout.tlsDir} is root:pipulse 2750 and ${ctx.layout.caRoot} root 0700`);
  return 0;
}

const exists = (path: string, whenUnsure: boolean) => {
  try {
    statSync(path);
    return true;
  } catch (error) {
    return (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT' ? false : whenUnsure;
  }
};

/**
 * First start, in order: recover a journal, stage and validate a CA and leaf
 * (names-only, or PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS; never inferred), activate them, write
 * state.json when absent, print the CA facts (the out-of-band channel,
 * identical on every start), and only then mark ready — the server waits
 * for that.
 */
export async function sidecarStart(ctx: Context, readyFile = READY_FILE): Promise<number> {
  rmSync(readyFile, { force: true });
  // The same lock as the CLI: `docker compose run pipulse-tls pipulse tls new-ca` may run meanwhile.
  const code = await withLock(
    ctx.layout,
    () => init(ctx, ['--yes', '--quiet']),
    lockOptions(ctx, true)
  );
  if (code !== 0) return code;
  if (readState(ctx.layout) === undefined) {
    const db = ctx.env['PIPULSE_DB_PATH']?.trim() || '/data/pipulse.sqlite';
    const mode = decideInitialMode({
      previousInstall: false,
      dbExists: exists(db, true),
      markerExists: exists(join(dirname(db), 'tls-installed'), false)
    });
    writeState(ctx.layout, mode, ctx.hook);
    ctx.out(
      mode === 'https'
        ? 'state.json: https'
        : 'state.json: legacy-http (this data comes from an HTTP install). HTTPS is ready: docker compose run --rm pipulse-tls pipulse tls enable --yes, then docker compose restart pipulse'
    );
  }
  printCa(ctx);
  const clock = ctx.clock();
  ctx.out(
    `clock: ${clock.state}${ctx.env['PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK']?.trim() === 'trust' ? ' (PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust)' : ''}`
  );
  writeFileSync(readyFile, `${ctx.now()}\n`, { mode: 0o600 });
  return 0;
}

const wait = (ms: number, signal?: AbortSignal) =>
  new Promise<boolean>((resolve) => {
    if (signal?.aborted) return resolve(true);
    const timer = setTimeout(() => resolve(false), ms);
    signal?.addEventListener(
      'abort',
      () => {
        clearTimeout(timer);
        resolve(true);
      },
      { once: true }
    );
  });

export async function sidecar(
  ctx: Context,
  args: string[],
  options: { readyFile?: string; intervalMs?: number; stop?: AbortSignal } = {}
): Promise<number> {
  usage(() => parseArgs({ args, options: {}, strict: true }));
  const code = await sidecarStart(ctx, options.readyFile);
  if (code !== 0) {
    ctx.err('pipulse-tls is not ready; the server will not start until this is fixed');
    return code;
  }
  for (;;) {
    try {
      await withLock(ctx.layout, () => renew(ctx, []), lockOptions(ctx, false));
    } catch (error) {
      ctx.err(`renewal skipped this round: ${(error as Error).message}`); // e.g. a new-ca is running
    }
    if (await wait(options.intervalMs ?? SIDECAR_INTERVAL_MS, options.stop)) return 0;
  }
}

export function sidecarHealth(ctx: Context, readyFile = READY_FILE): number {
  if (!existsSync(readyFile)) {
    ctx.err('not ready yet');
    return 1;
  }
  const ca = checkCa(paths(ctx.layout).caDir);
  if (ca.kind !== 'ok') {
    ctx.err(`no usable CA: ${ca.kind === 'partial' ? ca.problem : 'missing'}`);
    return 1;
  }
  const leaf = checkLeaf(paths(ctx.layout).bundle, ca.ca, pub(ctx.layout));
  if (leaf.kind !== 'ok' || validityOf(leaf, ctx.now(), 0) === 'expired') {
    ctx.err('no valid certificate');
    return 1;
  }
  const renewal = readRenewStatus(paths(ctx.layout).renewStatus);
  if (renewal?.result === 'failed') {
    ctx.err(`renewal failing: ${renewal.reason}`);
    return 1;
  }
  return 0;
}
```

In `cli.ts` add `'sidecar-init'` (→ `sidecarInit(ctx)`), `'sidecar'` (→ `sidecar(ctx, args)`), `'sidecar-health'` (→ `sidecarHealth(ctx)`, not root-only, so a container health check needs nothing extra).

### Task 25: Docker image, compose file and the compose test

**Files:**

- Modify: `Dockerfile`, `compose.yaml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`docker` job), `.gitignore` (`.compose-test.*`)
- Create: `packaging/test/compose.sh`

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: the image ships `openssl` and `/usr/bin/pipulse`, pins the `pipulse` UID/GID, and sets `PIPULSE_TLS_DIR=/tls`, `PIPULSE_TLS_CA_DIR=/tls-ca`, `PIPULSE_TLS_TIMESYNC_DIR=/host-timesync` and `PIPULSE_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/pipulse`. `compose.yaml` has the spec's three services, with two changes (ruling R10): the server gets the timesync mount too, because its health and certificate alerts read the clock; and the mount uses `create_host_path: false`, so a chrony host gets a clear error instead of a fake "unsynced forever" folder.

- [ ] **Step 1: Pin the UID**

Run `docker run --rm --entrypoint id ghcr.io/seviyon/pipulse:0.6.3 pipulse`. Use the printed uid/gid (expected `999`) in the Dockerfile. Pinning the same numbers keeps existing `pipulse-data` volumes readable. If they differ from 999, use the printed numbers everywhere below.

- [ ] **Step 2: `Dockerfile` (final stage)**

```dockerfile
FROM node:24-bookworm-slim@sha256:0e0ff40c39bc087845bfb27465a0df4ea419520094bc35842ff83dd8cbe6f9b6
# openssl: the sidecar issues certificates with it. The pipulse ids are pinned so the
# compose file's tmpfs options and existing volumes keep matching.
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends openssl && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/* \
 && groupadd --system --gid 999 pipulse && useradd --system --uid 999 --gid pipulse --home /data --shell /usr/sbin/nologin pipulse \
 && mkdir /data && chown pipulse:pipulse /data
COPY --from=build /app /opt/pipulse/app
COPY --chmod=755 packaging/pipulse /usr/bin/pipulse
ENV PIPULSE_DB_PATH=/data/pipulse.sqlite \
    PIPULSE_WEB_DIR=/opt/pipulse/app/packages/web/dist \
    PIPULSE_HOST_ROOT=/host \
    PIPULSE_IN_CONTAINER=true \
    PIPULSE_TLS_DIR=/tls \
    PIPULSE_TLS_CA_DIR=/tls-ca \
    PIPULSE_TLS_TIMESYNC_DIR=/host-timesync \
    PIPULSE_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/pipulse \
    NODE_OPTIONS=--disable-warning=ExperimentalWarning
USER pipulse
VOLUME /data
EXPOSE 8889
HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=5s --retries=3 --start-period=60s \
  CMD node /opt/pipulse/app/packages/tls/dist/health-check.js || exit 1
CMD ["node", "/opt/pipulse/app/packages/api/dist/server.js"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: `compose.yaml`**

```yaml
# PiPulse with HTTPS. A one-shot service prepares the volumes, the pipulse-tls
# sidecar holds the CA key and renews the certificate, and the server only
# ever reads the certificate. Settings go in ./pipulse.env (see packaging/pipulse.env).
# First start: `docker compose logs pipulse-tls` shows the CA fingerprint to check
# before you trust the CA (docker compose exec pipulse pipulse tls export-ca).
x-image: &image ghcr.io/seviyon/pipulse:latest@sha256:5342b43cbf51097cb316c509e695be36ff93310f5869f3b8ff7bad761d3a515b
x-timesync: &timesync
  # The host's clock signal (systemd-timesyncd). On a chrony or ntpd host, delete this
  # mount (here and below) and set PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust in pipulse.env.
  type: bind
  source: /run/systemd/timesync
  target: /host-timesync
  read_only: true
  bind: { create_host_path: false }

services:
  pipulse-tls-init: # one-shot: volume owners and modes (the only service with a capability)
    image: *image
    command: ['pipulse', 'tls', 'sidecar-init']
    user: '0:0'
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    cap_add: [CHOWN]
    security_opt: ['no-new-privileges:true']
    read_only: true
    network_mode: none
    restart: 'no'
    healthcheck: { disable: true }
    volumes: [tls-ca:/tls-ca, tls:/tls]

  pipulse-tls: # the CA, the certificate and its renewal; opens no sockets
    image: *image
    command: ['pipulse', 'tls', 'sidecar']
    user: '0:0'
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    security_opt: ['no-new-privileges:true']
    read_only: true
    tmpfs: [/tmp]
    network_mode: host # the host's hostname and default-route address
    env_file:
      - path: ./pipulse.env
        required: false
    volumes:
      - tls-ca:/tls-ca # the CA key: this service only
      - tls:/tls # certificate, state, metadata
      - pipulse-data:/data:ro # only to tell an upgrade (existing data) from a fresh install
      - *timesync
    depends_on:
      pipulse-tls-init: { condition: service_completed_successfully }
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD', 'pipulse', 'tls', 'sidecar-health']
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 120s
    restart: unless-stopped

  pipulse:
    image: *image
    restart: unless-stopped
    network_mode: host # the host's network counters and hostname; set PIPULSE_PORT in pipulse.env
    cap_drop: [ALL]
    security_opt: ['no-new-privileges:true']
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp
      - /run/pipulse:uid=999,gid=999,mode=0750
    env_file:
      - path: ./pipulse.env # your settings (see packaging/pipulse.env); optional until you need one
        required: false
    volumes:
      - pipulse-data:/data
      - tls:/tls:ro
      - *timesync
      - ./config:/etc/pipulse:ro # password hash, rules, notify file, CA certificate
      - /boot/firmware:/boot/firmware:ro # the /boot usage tile (use /boot on older Pi OS)
      - /etc/os-release:/host/etc/os-release:ro # the host's OS name, not the image's
      - /proc/device-tree/model:/host/model:ro # the Pi model (Docker hides /sys/firmware)
    depends_on:
      pipulse-tls: { condition: service_healthy }

volumes:
  pipulse-data:
  tls:
  tls-ca:
```

- [ ] **Step 4: `packaging/test/compose.sh`**

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# compose.sh IMAGE — the compose deployment: healthy, owners and modes, the server can't
# see the CA or write /tls, the marker, a stable CA, capabilities, a failing or stopped
# sidecar never stops the server, the clock gate, and an existing volume staying on HTTP.
set -eu
image=$1
repo=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/../.." && pwd)
work=$(mktemp -d "$repo/.compose-test.XXXXXX") # inside the repo: Colima shares only the home folder
fail=0
ok() { echo "ok - $1"; }
bad() { echo "not ok - $1"; fail=1; }
mkdir -p "$work/timesync" "$work/config" && touch "$work/timesync/synchronized"
printf 'PIPULSE_PORT=18891\n' > "$work/pipulse.env"
# The test compose file: the test image, a fake clock signal, no Pi-only host files.
sed -e "s#ghcr.io/seviyon/pipulse:[^ ]*#$image#" -e "s#/run/systemd/timesync#$work/timesync#" \
  -e '/\/boot\/firmware/d' -e '/os-release/d' -e '/device-tree/d' "$repo/compose.yaml" > "$work/compose.yaml"
dc() { docker compose -p pipulse-test -f "$work/compose.yaml" "$@"; }
trap 'dc down -v >/dev/null 2>&1 || true; rm -rf "$work"' EXIT

dc up -d >/dev/null 2>&1
healthy() { [ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.State.Health.Status}}' "$(dc ps -q "$1")" 2>/dev/null)" = healthy ]; }
for _ in $(seq 1 120); do healthy pipulse && break; sleep 1; done
healthy pipulse && ok 'compose up: the server is healthy' || { dc logs; bad 'compose up: the server is healthy'; }
healthy pipulse-tls && ok 'the sidecar is healthy' || bad 'the sidecar is healthy'
[ "$(dc exec -T pipulse-tls stat -c '%U:%G %a' /tls /tls-ca /tls-ca/ca/ca.key /tls/leaf.pem | tr '\n' ' ')" = 'root:pipulse 2750 root:root 700 root:root 600 root:pipulse 640 ' ] && ok 'volume owners and modes' || bad "volume owners and modes ($(dc exec -T pipulse-tls stat -c '%n %U:%G %a' /tls /tls-ca /tls-ca/ca/ca.key /tls/leaf.pem | tr '\n' ' '))"
dc exec -T pipulse test ! -e /tls-ca && ok 'the server has no CA volume' || bad 'the server has no CA volume'
if dc exec -T pipulse touch /tls/intruder 2>/dev/null; then bad 'the server cannot write /tls'; else ok 'the server cannot write /tls'; fi
dc exec -T pipulse cat /tls/leaf.pem >/dev/null && ok 'the server reads leaf.pem' || bad 'the server reads leaf.pem'
dc exec -T pipulse test -e /data/tls-installed && ok 'the server marked the data as served over HTTPS' || bad 'tls-installed marker'
dc exec -T pipulse node /opt/pipulse/app/packages/tls/dist/health-check.js && ok 'verified HTTPS from inside' || bad 'verified HTTPS from inside'
dc exec -T pipulse pipulse tls status | grep -q 'HTTPS, pid' && ok 'status sees the running server' || bad 'status sees the running server'
[ "$(dc exec -T pipulse-tls grep CapEff /proc/1/status | awk '{print $2}')" = 0000000000000000 ] && ok 'the sidecar has no capabilities' || bad 'the sidecar has no capabilities'
[ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.HostConfig.CapAdd}}' "$(dc ps -aq pipulse-tls-init)")" = '[CHOWN]' ] && ok 'the init service has only CHOWN' || bad 'the init service has only CHOWN'
fp1=$(dc logs pipulse-tls | sed -n 's/.*CA SHA-256 fingerprint: //p' | head -n 1)
dc restart pipulse-tls >/dev/null 2>&1
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do healthy pipulse-tls && break; sleep 1; done
[ "$(dc logs pipulse-tls | sed -n 's/.*CA SHA-256 fingerprint: //p' | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" = 1 ] && [ -n "$fp1" ] && ok 'a restart keeps and reprints the same CA' || bad 'a restart keeps and reprints the same CA'

# A failing sidecar (unreadable CA key: root has no DAC override there) doesn't touch the server.
dc exec -T pipulse-tls chmod 000 /tls-ca/ca/ca.key
dc exec -T pipulse-tls pipulse tls renew --force >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
if dc exec -T pipulse-tls pipulse tls sidecar-health >/dev/null 2>&1; then bad 'a failing renewal makes the sidecar unhealthy'; else ok 'a failing renewal makes the sidecar unhealthy'; fi
restarts=$(docker inspect -f '{{.RestartCount}}' "$(dc ps -q pipulse)")
dc exec -T pipulse node /opt/pipulse/app/packages/tls/dist/health-check.js && ok 'the server keeps serving' || bad 'the server keeps serving'
dc exec -T pipulse-tls chmod 600 /tls-ca/ca/ca.key
dc stop pipulse-tls >/dev/null 2>&1
dc exec -T pipulse node /opt/pipulse/app/packages/tls/dist/health-check.js && ok 'a stopped sidecar leaves the server serving' || bad 'a stopped sidecar leaves the server serving'
[ "$(docker inspect -f '{{.RestartCount}}' "$(dc ps -q pipulse)")" = "$restarts" ] && ok 'and compose never restarted it' || bad 'and compose never restarted it'
dc start pipulse-tls >/dev/null 2>&1

# No clock signal: renewal waits; PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust lets it renew.
rm "$work/timesync/synchronized"
dc exec -T pipulse-tls pipulse tls renew --force | grep -q 'waiting for clock synchronization' && ok 'an unsynced clock makes renewal wait' || bad 'an unsynced clock makes renewal wait'
rmdir "$work/timesync" 2>/dev/null || true
dc exec -T -e PIPULSE_TLS_TIMESYNC_DIR=/nonexistent -e PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust pipulse-tls pipulse tls renew --force | grep -q '^renewed' && ok 'PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust renews with no signal' || bad 'PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust renews with no signal'
dc down -v >/dev/null 2>&1
mkdir -p "$work/timesync" && touch "$work/timesync/synchronized"

# An existing data volume (an HTTP install) stays on HTTP, with HTTPS prepared.
docker volume create pipulse-test_pipulse-data >/dev/null
docker run --rm -u 0 -v pipulse-test_pipulse-data:/data --entrypoint sh "$image" -c 'touch /data/pipulse.sqlite && chown -R 999:999 /data'
dc up -d >/dev/null 2>&1
for _ in $(seq 1 120); do healthy pipulse && break; sleep 1; done
dc exec -T pipulse-tls cat /tls/state.json | grep -q legacy-http && ok 'existing data stays on HTTP' || bad 'existing data stays on HTTP'
dc logs pipulse | grep -q 'HTTPS is ready: docker compose run --rm pipulse-tls pipulse tls enable --yes' && ok 'the server says how to enable HTTPS' || bad 'the server says how to enable HTTPS'
exit $fail
```

## Carried forward from the Task 1 review

- `issue.ts` now refuses a `/0` prefix and an address with host bits set (`192.168.77.5/24`); Task 2's `constraints.ts` must still hand it only masked network addresses, and test that.
- The `pipulse tls` entry points that issue (`init`, `renew`, `new-ca`, `restore-ca`) must call `opensslVersion()` first, so a LibreSSL or missing `openssl` fails with its own message, not a generic `openssl ca` error (Tasks 5, 8, 10).
- A SIGKILL between `mkdtempSync` and `finally` in `issue.ts` leaves a root-only `issue-*` folder holding `key.pem` in `workDir`. The journal's recovery task (Task 6) must sweep stale `issue-*` folders, and its crash test should cover it.

- `cleanTemp` has no age check and no lock of its own. Only mutating commands may call it, and only after taking the interprocess lock (Task 6's lock, Task 8's commands). `status`, `export-ca` and the other read-only commands must not call it, or they could delete a running writer's temp file and make its rename fail.
- `settingsEnv` now throws on an unreadable `pipulse.env` unless the environment already has `PIPULSE_TLS` (EACCES/EPERM) or the file is simply missing (ENOENT). The CLI entry (Task 10) must let that one-line error end the command with a non-zero exit, never catch and continue. Whether the renew unit's sandbox shows `pipulse.env` as ENOENT or EACCES is unchecked: confirm it in Task 12 when the unit is written, and adjust the tolerated codes if needed.

- Task 4's "certificate N is not a CA certificate" check relies on Node's `X509Certificate.ca`, which (unconfirmed) is `X509_check_ca() == 1`: an operator chain ending in a very old v1 self-signed root with no basic-constraints extension would be refused. Rare, since most operators leave the root out of the served chain. If an operator reports it, relax the check for a self-signed final certificate.

- `checkLeaf` never judges dates: an expired leaf returns `ok` with a past `notAfter` (tested). Task 8's `renew` and `status` must compare `notBefore`/`notAfter` with the clock themselves and say "expired" or "not yet valid" in their own words.
- `backupName(now, taken)` moves to the next free second; Tasks 6 and 9 must pass `taken` (`name => existsSync(join(caRoot, name))`) wherever a backup is named, or two `new-ca` runs in one second collide.
- `checkCa`/`checkLeaf` now return `partial`/`refused` (never `none`/`missing`) for any stat error other than ENOENT. Tasks 8 and 10 must treat `partial` as "stop and show", never as permission to make a CA.
- `constraints.json` is bound to the CA only by fingerprint, and Node can't read a certificate's name-constraints extension, so nothing proves the file matches what the CA enforces. Root-only, low risk. Option for Task 8 or 13: after `init`/`renew`, a loopback handshake of the new leaf against the CA, as in Task 1's matrix, flagging a mismatch.

- The lock records a token, boot id, PID namespace and host, and heartbeats its mtime every 20 s. A holder in another PID namespace (the Docker sidecar and a `docker compose run` command share the `tls-ca` volume) is judged by that heartbeat (stale after 90 s), never by its pid. Task 24's sidecar must hold the lock through `withLock` like any command, and its loop must not block the event loop for 90 s at a time, or its heartbeat stops. Breaking a stale lock renames it aside and re-reads it, but the last window (between that rename and `link()` putting a fresh lock back) is not closed; the journal keeps the CA consistent regardless.

- Task 8's R15 test (`cli-init.test.ts`, "refuses init, naming the setting and the reason") lists only `init`, because `new-ca`, `restore-ca` and `enable` don't exist yet. Task 9 adds `['new-ca', '--yes']` and `['restore-ca', 'ca.old-20260101T000000Z', '--yes']` to that list, and Task 10 adds `['enable', '--yes']`; each must exit 1 with the same message.

- `renew` no longer treats the leaf's expiry as a reason once the leaf ends where the CA ends (it can't be extended): it records `not-due` with "valid until <date>, when the CA expires: sudo pipulse tls new-ca". Nothing else warns about an expiring CA, so Task 16's expiry alerts must cover the CA itself, not just the leaf.
- `renew` waits a day (`IPV6_RENEW_AFTER_MS`) before a new IPv6 address makes the leaf due, because privacy addresses rotate and Node's `networkInterfaces()` can't tell them apart; IPv4 is immediate. Unconfirmed on a real IPv6 LAN: check in Task 13 whether the SAN list or reload frequency follows address rotation.

- `new-ca` takes several `--prune-oldest` values (after an interrupted prune there can be three backups, and the new one must still leave two); it keeps the current CA's subnets unless `--subnet` or `PIPULSE_TLS_SUBNETS` says otherwise (`--subnet none` drops them); and `buildConstraints` refuses any single-label name except the host and `localhost` (a dotted alias such as `pi.local` is the way), so a CA can't be made permitted for a whole TLD. Task 12's README must say so, and Task 22's installer prompt must not offer single-label names.

- `enable` and `disable` write `state.json` without the journal: if the CLI is killed between the write and the health check (or its rollback), the server may be left restarting in the new mode. The installer's and operator docs' recovery is the same one line either way (`sudo pipulse tls disable --yes`, or delete `state.json`); Task 12's README must give it. `enable` refuses an expired or not-yet-valid generated leaf (checkLeaf doesn't judge dates) and only warns for an operator certificate that has expired. `enable` and `disable` run `recover()` only where they may write: not on a refused native folder, not for an operator pair.

- The lock never breaks a holder whose pid and start time verify, whatever its age (commands hold it across `confirm()` and the clock-sync wait, and the wall clock jumps on a Pi when NTP syncs); the age bound applies only to a holder whose identity can't be verified (no /proc). A holder in another PID namespace looks gone after 90 s without a heartbeat, but is only broken once its mtime then stays unchanged for 45 s on the waiter's monotonic clock, and a waiter watching one is not cut short by `waitMs`. The cost: an operator who leaves `new-ca` at its prompt holds the lock, so the hourly renew fails with "another pipulse tls command is running" until they answer (a 30-day margin, retried hourly); Task 12's README should say so. Task 24's sidecar must hold the lock the same way.
- `enable`/`disable` lock with `create: true` and call `ensureTlsDirs` before writing `state.json` (no TLS folder exists after a 6b-1 install with an operator pair). `renew` with no CA but a served `leaf.pem` or a `state.json` records `failed`. `repairPublic` rewrites `leaf.crt` from `leaf.pem`'s certificate when a kill left them apart (renew runs it hourly).

- `renew` counts only a served `leaf.pem` or a `state.json` that says `https` (or can't be read) as proof a CA should exist; `legacy-http` or no state with no CA is "not set up" (exit 0, nothing recorded), because the hourly timer runs on every install and Task 22 makes upgrades write `legacy-http`. A `LockError` from `renew` (a `new-ca` left at its prompt for longer than the 30 s wait) is recorded as `failed` with the lock message. `setup.sh` re-enables a disabled renewal timer on every upgrade (a disabled unit can't be told from a new one); that is accepted and noted for the README.

**Interface consistency (tasks that share files or types):**

- T5 ↔ T6: the journal imports `StageDirs` (type only), `paths`, `MODES`, `pub`/`priv` and `backupName` from `layout.ts`/`material.ts`; `validateStage` is what every `runTransaction` caller passes as `validate`. Stage folders are split per filesystem (`<caRoot>/ca.next-*`, `<tlsDir>/.next-*`) so Docker's two volumes work (tested in T24).
- T5 ↔ T7–T10, T24: `Context` (T7) is structurally an `IssueContext` (T5); every command uses the same `recover` → `prepareWork` → work → `removeTree(work)` sequence.
- T2 ↔ T8/T10/T22: `buildConstraints` refuses hostile input before any CA is made; `leafNames` decides both what a leaf carries and when `renew` is due (`renewDue` compares against it), so the two can't disagree. T22's `init --mode auto` reuses T8's material path unchanged.
- T14 ↔ T15/T18: the status file (`RuntimeStatus`) is only for the CLI. `/api/health` and `/api/config` read the provider directly plus `readGeneratedExtras` (T15), and Settings reads `/api/config` (T18). The three never parse each other's output.
- T6 ↔ T15: the journal installs `ca.crt` and `ca-meta.json` before `leaf.pem`, so for up to about two minutes the metadata describes a CA the server doesn't serve yet. T15 shows `ca-meta.json` only when its `fingerprint` equals the served certificate's `caFingerprint` (set by `loadCertificate` from `ca.crt` when the pair was loaded). Otherwise `ca.state` is `transitional`, with no scope and no coverage. Tested before and after the reload.
- T5 ↔ T15: `readGeneratedExtras` uses T5's `parseMeta`/`parseRenewStatus`, whose error messages name the file, never a path or content. Only a missing `renew-status.json` is quiet. Every other read or parse failure is a `MetadataProblem` (config, read-protected) and the `generated-metadata-unreadable` reason (public health, enum only).
- T15 ↔ T18: `TlsView` in `packages/api/src/tls-status.ts` is mirrored in `packages/web/src/types.ts` (`inContainer`, `ca` with its `state`, `coverage`, `renewal`, `metadata`, `metadataProblems`). Settings renders one line for `transitional` and one per problem.
- T7 ↔ T8–T10, T11, T12, T24: `Context.defaultTlsDir` and `nativeLayoutProblem` (T7) gate `init`, `renew` (which records `failed`), `new-ca`, `restore-ca` and `enable`, and `status` prints the problem. That keeps native generated material inside the one folder the T11 renew unit can write (`ReadWritePaths=/etc/pipulse/tls`). The sidecar (T24) runs with `inContainer`, so Docker's `/tls` and `/tls-ca` are allowed. Operator certificates skip the check. The packaging line is in T12.
- T6 ↔ T7, T24, T11: `withLock` (Task 6) is taken by `main` (Task 7) for `init`, `new-ca`, `restore-ca`, `renew`, `enable` and `disable`, before `recover()`. The sidecar (Task 24) takes it around its `init` and every `renew`. The hourly timer runs `pipulse tls renew` through `main`, so it can't race a manual `new-ca`. A refused native layout or an operator certificate runs unlocked, because nothing is written. The renew unit's `BindPaths=-`/`ReadWritePaths=-` (Task 11) let it start on a Pi with no TLS folder yet. `processIdentity` moved from Task 14 to Task 6's `proc.ts` (Task 14 re-exports it).
- T4 ↔ T15: `caFingerprint` is the anchor the served chain verified against (generated sources only). Task 15's `transitional` state covers both an interrupted rotation and the reloader republishing a new `ca.crt` with the old leaf (then `caFingerprint` is `undefined`).
- T21 ↔ T22: the server's `writeTlsMarker` result is logged as `ERROR`, shown in config, health and `status`, and required by `setup.sh`'s first-install gate.
- T22 ↔ `install.sh`: `/etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending` makes every retry of a failed fresh install a first install (`--first-install` on all three setup paths). Only `setup.sh`'s success gate removes it.
- T8 ↔ T11: a refused native `renew` records `failed` in the default folder, the one the unit can write, and always exits 1.
- T1 Step 7b ↔ T2 Step 6: the D2 switch `EXCLUDE_BELOW_SINGLE_LABEL` stays `false` until the browser results are recorded in the plan. Tests pass the option explicitly for both positions.
- T16 ↔ T17: `CERTIFICATE_METRIC` is the value in `alerts.metric` and in the notify label list; `ruleToEntry`/`parseRuleEntry` round-trip through the editor's `bodyOf`/`draftOf`.
- T7 `RELEASE_DEFAULT` ↔ T21: one constant, read by the server, `checkHealth` and the CLI's `status`/`enable`.
- T21 marker ↔ T22/T24: the server writes `tls-installed` next to `PIPULSE_DB_PATH`; `decideInitialMode` reads it natively (`setup.sh` → `init --mode auto`) and in Docker (`sidecarStart`).

## Spec corrections needed

1. **IP exclusions (Name constraints).** `excludedSubtrees 0.0.0.0/0` and `::/0` exclude everything, including `127.0.0.1` and accepted subnets, because an exclusion beats every permit (verified with OpenSSL 1.1.1w and 3.6.4). Correct it to: no IP exclusions. The permitted list always holds `127.0.0.1/32` and `::1/128`, so IP SANs are already confined to it.
2. **Single-label host names.** `permitted;DNS:io` permits all of `*.io` (RFC 5280 suffix matching, verified). Whether to add `excluded;DNS:.<name>` for single-label names is D2. It sits behind the `EXCLUDE_BELOW_SINGLE_LABEL` switch, off until the Task 1 Step 7b browser results are recorded. The spec should state the risk and the gate.
3. **Renewal timer.** Hourly (`OnCalendar=hourly`, `RandomizedDelaySec=15min`), not daily with 6 h, so a renewal waiting for the clock after a reboot runs within about an hour. Add `ReadWritePaths=/etc/pipulse/tls` and `ProtectHome=yes` to the renew unit (checked on Io, Task 13 Step 10).
4. **Serials and CA subject.** Random 128-bit serials, and a CA subject with a random install id (`PiPulse CA <host> <6 hex>`), so Firefox never sees two certificates with the same issuer and serial, and two Pis with the same host name don't collide.
5. **Native legacy detection.** The `tls-installed` marker applies natively too (`/var/lib/pipulse/tls-installed`). An install is an upgrade if there was a previous `version.json` **or** a database, and no marker. That covers a reinstall over data kept by `--uninstall`.
6. **`PIPULSE_TLS_CA_DIR`** (Docker `/tls-ca`, native = `PIPULSE_TLS_DIR`). Journal stages are split per filesystem, because a rename can't cross volumes. Add it to the settings table.
7. **Issuance clock gate.** `init`, `new-ca`, `restore-ca` and the sidecar's first start accept an `unknown` clock past `CLOCK_FLOOR_MS`, with a warning. `renew` stays strict. Without this, a chrony host or a container without the timesync mount could never make its first CA.
8. **Release default.** Under `refuse`, a configured `PIPULSE_TLS_CERT` + `PIPULSE_TLS_KEY` pair means HTTPS; `state.json` still wins over it. The fix line becomes `REFUSE_LINE` (it also names `sudo pipulse tls init`).
9. **Plain HTTP on the HTTPS port.** From "out of scope" to "a fixed 400 hint page, sent on `tlsClientError`/`ERR_SSL_HTTP_REQUEST` without reading the request" (only if D1 is yes).
10. **Docker clock.** The server container also mounts the timesync folder (its health and certificate alerts read the clock). The mount uses `create_host_path: false`: chrony/ntpd hosts delete it and set `PIPULSE_TLS_CLOCK=trust`, rather than get an empty folder that reads as "unsynced" forever.
11. **Docker `enable`/`disable`.** In a container these only write `state.json` and ask for `docker compose restart pipulse` (a container can't restart its sibling).
12. **`/api/health` `renewal` values.** `ok | waiting-clock | failing | unknown` (unknown = no renewal recorded yet, not degraded); omitted for operator certificates; `failing` adds reason `renewal-failing`.
13. **`PIPULSE_RUNTIME_DIR`** (default `/run/pipulse`; the unit's `RuntimeDirectory=pipulse`, Docker's tmpfs). The status file gains `transport`, and `certificate` is `null` over HTTP.
14. **Certificate alerts.** They use the pseudo-metric `certificate` in `alerts.metric` (no migration). `ruleHash` appends certificate fields only when present, so no existing alert closes as `rule_changed` on upgrade.
15. **Sidecar socket test.** The module-graph test allows `node:net` imported only for `isIP`, and `node:tls` (for `rootCertificates`). The real guarantee is the `strace` test.
16. **Fresh-install failure.** In the `.deb` path, a failed health check on a first install fails `postinst` (dpkg keeps it half-configured; `apt-get -f install` reruns setup), per the spec's "stops"; upgrades never fail on health in `postinst`.
17. **Metadata of the served CA only.** `/api/config` shows `ca-meta.json` only when its fingerprint equals the served certificate's CA (`ca.state`: `ok | transitional | unavailable`). `/api/health`'s `certificate` gains `metadata: ok | transitional | unreadable`. `unreadable` (any read error but a missing `renew-status.json`, or any parse failure) adds the reason `generated-metadata-unreadable` (degraded). File names and messages appear only in `/api/config` (`metadataProblems`).
18. **The generated-certificate folder on a native install.** Only `/etc/pipulse/tls` (the renewal unit's `ReadWritePaths=`). `pipulse tls` refuses a non-default `PIPULSE_TLS_DIR` and any `PIPULSE_TLS_CA_DIR` natively; containers set their own folders. The settings table should say so.
19. **One `pipulse tls` command at a time.** An interprocess lock (`<caRoot>/.pipulse-tls.lock`, `O_EXCL`, pid + start time, stale after the holder dies or 15 min) is taken before journal recovery by every command that changes material or mode, the timer's `renew` and the sidecar included.
20. **A failed fresh install stays fresh.** `install.sh`'s `/etc/pipulse/.first-install-pending` marker makes every retry a first install, and only `setup.sh`'s success gate removes it. The success gate includes the `tls-installed` marker, and a server that can't write that marker says so (log `ERROR`, health `tls-marker-unwritten`, config, `status`) instead of warning quietly.
21. **The renew unit on a Pi with no TLS folder.** `BindPaths=-/etc/pipulse/tls` and `ReadWritePaths=-/etc/pipulse/tls` (a missing source is ignored), so the hourly unit starts and reports "nothing to renew" rather than failing.

## Rulings made while planning

- **R1** Issuance uses the `openssl` CLI as the spec says; the DER encoder is only the fallback if Task 1 fails. `/usr/bin/openssl` is a fixed path, the `.deb` depends on `openssl`, and the image installs it.
- **R2** Keys are generated with `node:crypto` (PKCS#8 guaranteed); OpenSSL only signs, in a `0700` work folder on the CA's filesystem that is emptied before and after.
- **R3** Issuance clock gate as in correction 7.
- **R4** Hourly renewal checks (correction 3). This was D3; the user raised no objection, so it is a ruling, which the user can still overrule.
- **R5** The timer is enabled on every install and upgrade unless masked; masking is the documented way to turn renewal off.
- **R6** `renew` is also due early for a covered `PIPULSE_TLS_NAMES` name missing from the leaf (same rule as a missing default-route address).
- **R7** The migration table in Task 22 (correction 5). Unreadable paths lean toward "stay on HTTP".
- **R8** Cert + key imply HTTPS under `refuse` (correction 8).
- **R9** `pipulse tls` never sources `pipulse.env`: it runs as root and reads it as data (`settingsEnv`). The process environment wins, so the renew unit's `EnvironmentFile=` still applies.
- **R10** Docker clock mounts as in correction 10; the image pins the `pipulse` UID/GID (expected 999, checked first).
- **R11** The CA certificate is exported through the CLI only (`export-ca`, `--out` owned by `SUDO_UID`), never downloadable from the web page: the fingerprint check must not rely on the channel it verifies.
- **R12** `tls.checkServerIdentity` can't match IPv6 literals (Node 22.23 and 24.19): the health check and tests use `X509Certificate.checkIP` for IP identities.
- **R13** Three PRs: 6b-2a `release:patch` (v0.6.4), 6b-2b `release:patch` (v0.6.5), 6b-2c `release:minor` (v0.7.0). Renewal ships in 6b-2a so an opt-in leaf is never left without renewal.
- **R14** The hint page for `http://` on the HTTPS port (Task 19) is built. This was D1; the user raised no objection, and can still overrule it (then skip Task 19). It sends a fixed 400 page only after OpenSSL flags an HTTP request: no parsing, no Host echo, no second port. It relies on Node's undocumented `tlsSocket._parent` (checked on Node 24.19) and falls back to today's dropped connection.
- **R15** A native install keeps generated material only in `/etc/pipulse/tls` (the coordinator's ruling, kept unchanged). `init`, `renew`, `new-ca`, `restore-ca` and `enable` (generated source) refuse other folders with a message naming the setting and the reason. `renew` also records `failed`, and `status` prints the problem. Containers are exempt. Operator certificates are unaffected: they are never written. I considered making the unit's `ReadWritePaths=` follow the setting instead, but a unit file can't read `pipulse.env`, and a drop-in written by setup would widen root's writable area on every install for one rare setting.

## Decisions for the user

- **D2 — Exclude names below a single-label host (`excluded;DNS:.io`). Gated, not decided by planning.** It changes every new CA's DNS scope for good. The code ships with the switch off (Task 2). The controller runs the browser session in Task 1 Step 7 (`io` must load and `x.io` must be refused, in Chrome, Firefox and Safari) and records the results in Task 1 Step 7b. Task 2 Step 6 turns the switch on only if every browser behaved. Cost: one manual browser session, before any CA is made on Io. Left off, a stolen CA key could vouch for any name below a host name that is a real TLD (such as `io`), and the README says so.
- **D4 — Only if the `openssl` spike fails on 1.1.1:** I recommend the hand-written DER encoder (about 350 lines, same interface) over dropping Pi OS 11 support. Cost: security-relevant ASN.1 code of our own, which needs its own review.
